===== M0947 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeUtil.checkForStateChangeHelper(Node,boolean,AbstractCompiler)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeUtil.java
Original range: 793-938
Documentation:
/**
   * Returns true if some node in n's subtree changes application state.
   * If {@code checkForNewObjects} is true, we assume that newly created
   * mutable objects (like object literals) change state. Otherwise, we assume
   * that they have no side effects.
   */
Method code:
793 |   private static boolean checkForStateChangeHelper(
794 |       Node n, boolean checkForNewObjects, AbstractCompiler compiler) {
795 |     // Rather than id which ops may have side effects, id the ones
796 |     // that we know to be safe
797 |     switch (n.getType()) {
798 |       // other side-effect free statements and expressions
799 |       case Token.CAST:
800 |       case Token.AND:
801 |       case Token.BLOCK:
802 |       case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
803 |       case Token.HOOK:
804 |       case Token.IF:
805 |       case Token.IN:
806 |       case Token.PARAM_LIST:
807 |       case Token.NUMBER:
808 |       case Token.OR:
809 |       case Token.THIS:
810 |       case Token.TRUE:
811 |       case Token.FALSE:
812 |       case Token.NULL:
813 |       case Token.STRING:
814 |       case Token.STRING_KEY:
815 |       case Token.SWITCH:
816 |       case Token.TRY:
817 |       case Token.EMPTY:
818 |         break;
819 | 
820 |       // Throws are by definition side effects
821 |       case Token.THROW:
822 |         return true;
823 | 
824 |       case Token.OBJECTLIT:
825 |         if (checkForNewObjects) {
826 |           return true;
827 |         }
828 |         for (Node c = n.getFirstChild(); c != null; c = c.getNext()) {
829 |           if (checkForStateChangeHelper(
830 |                   c.getFirstChild(), checkForNewObjects, compiler)) {
831 |             return true;
832 |           }
833 |         }
834 |         return false;
835 | 
836 |       case Token.ARRAYLIT:
837 |       case Token.REGEXP:
838 |         if (checkForNewObjects) {
839 |           return true;
840 |         }
841 |         break;
842 | 
843 |       case Token.VAR:    // empty var statement (no declaration)
844 |       case Token.NAME:   // variable by itself
845 |         if (n.getFirstChild() != null) {
846 |           return true;
847 |         }
848 |         break;
849 | 
850 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
851 |         // Function expressions don't have side-effects, but function
852 |         // declarations change the namespace. Either way, we don't need to
853 |         // check the children, since they aren't executed at declaration time.
854 |         return checkForNewObjects || !isFunctionExpression(n);
855 | 
856 |       case Token.NEW:
857 |         if (checkForNewObjects) {
858 |           return true;
859 |         }
860 | 
861 |         if (!constructorCallHasSideEffects(n)) {
862 |           // loop below will see if the constructor parameters have
863 |           // side-effects
864 |           break;
865 |         }
866 |         return true;
867 | 
868 |       case Token.CALL:
869 |         // calls to functions that have no side effects have the no
870 |         // side effect property set.
871 |         if (!functionCallHasSideEffects(n, compiler)) {
872 |           // loop below will see if the function parameters have
873 |           // side-effects
874 |           break;
875 |         }
876 |         return true;
877 | 
878 |       default:
879 |         if (isSimpleOperator(n)) {
880 |           break;
881 |         }
882 | 
883 |         if (isAssignmentOp(n)) {
884 |           Node assignTarget = n.getFirstChild();
885 |           if (assignTarget.isName()) {
886 |             return true;
887 |           }
888 | 
889 |           // Assignments will have side effects if
890 |           // a) The RHS has side effects, or
891 |           // b) The LHS has side effects, or
892 |           // c) A name on the LHS will exist beyond the life of this statement.
893 |           if (checkForStateChangeHelper(
894 |                   n.getFirstChild(), checkForNewObjects, compiler) ||
895 |               checkForStateChangeHelper(
896 |                   n.getLastChild(), checkForNewObjects, compiler)) {
897 |             return true;
898 |           }
899 | 
900 |           if (isGet(assignTarget)) {
901 |             // If the object being assigned to is a local object, don't
902 |             // consider this a side-effect as it can't be referenced
903 |             // elsewhere.  Don't do this recursively as the property might
904 |             // be an alias of another object, unlike a literal below.
905 |             Node current = assignTarget.getFirstChild();
906 |             if (evaluatesToLocalValue(current)) {
907 |               return false;
908 |             }
909 | 
910 |             // A literal value as defined by "isLiteralValue" is guaranteed
911 |             // not to be an alias, or any components which are aliases of
912 |             // other objects.
913 |             // If the root object is a literal don't consider this a
914 |             // side-effect.
915 |             while (isGet(current)) {
916 |               current = current.getFirstChild();
917 |             }
918 | 
919 |             return !isLiteralValue(current, true);
920 |           } else {
921 |             // TODO(johnlenz): remove this code and make this an exception. This
922 |             // is here only for legacy reasons, the AST is not valid but
923 |             // preserve existing behavior.
924 |             return !isLiteralValue(assignTarget, true);
925 |           }
926 |         }
927 | 
928 |         return true;
929 |     }
930 | 
931 |     for (Node c = n.getFirstChild(); c != null; c = c.getNext()) {
932 |       if (checkForStateChangeHelper(c, checkForNewObjects, compiler)) {
933 |         return true;
934 |       }
935 |     }
936 | 
937 |     return false;
938 |   }

===== M0955 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.process(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 148-190
Documentation:

Method code:
148 |   @Override
149 |   public void process(Node externs, Node root) {
150 |     this.root = root;
151 |     astPositionCounter = 0;
152 |     astPosition = Maps.newHashMap();
153 |     nodePriorities = Maps.newHashMap();
154 |     cfg = new AstControlFlowGraph(computeFallThrough(root), nodePriorities,
155 |                                   edgeAnnotations);
156 |     NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, this);
157 |     astPosition.put(null, ++astPositionCounter); // the implicit return is last.
158 | 
159 |     // Now, generate the priority of nodes by doing a depth-first
160 |     // search on the CFG.
161 |     priorityCounter = 0;
162 |     DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry = cfg.getEntry();
163 |     prioritizeFromEntryNode(entry);
164 | 
165 |     if (shouldTraverseFunctions) {
166 |       // If we're traversing inner functions, we need to rank the
167 |       // priority of them too.
168 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
169 |         Node value = candidate.getValue();
170 |         if (value != null && value.isFunction()) {
171 |           Preconditions.checkState(
172 |               !nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate) || candidate == entry);
173 |           prioritizeFromEntryNode(candidate);
174 |         }
175 |       }
176 |     }
177 | 
178 |     // At this point, all reachable nodes have been given a priority, but
179 |     // unreachable nodes have not been given a priority. Put them last.
180 |     // Presumably, it doesn't really matter what priority they get, since
181 |     // this shouldn't happen in real code.
182 |     for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
183 |       if (!nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate)) {
184 |         nodePriorities.put(candidate, ++priorityCounter);
185 |       }
186 |     }
187 | 
188 |     // Again, the implicit return node is always last.
189 |     nodePriorities.put(cfg.getImplicitReturn(), ++priorityCounter);
190 |   }
